  - **The fix is two lines of prose and two bytes of vector.** §9.1 now has a `field count` row that says what each production allows and admits, in the row itself, that it was doing the other rule's work. `bad-vertical-tab` and `bad-carriage-return` moved their stray character to `quorum\vpi` — *between a two-field directive's two fields*, where reading it as a separator gives `quorum pi`, a policy that parses. The new test says exactly that and nothing else: substitute an ASCII space and the text must parse, or some other rule is doing the refusing.
  - **D10: three readings of one sentence, all of them green.** "First non-space character" was the only row in that table that did not say "space or tab", and it said nothing about a `#` further along the line. `comment-tab-indented` (a tab-indented comment MUST be ignored) kills the U+0020-only reading; `bad-trailing-comment` (`quorum pi # note`) kills strip-from-the-first-`#`, because a `#` that is an ordinary character makes that a `quorum` line with two names; `witness-url-fragment` kills the reading where `#` is illegal in a field at all.
  - **The two defects are one defect.** `bad-trailing-comment` is only refusable *because* the field count is now a rule — drop the arity check and the comment vector goes green along with the arity one. The control run shows it: four variants, four reds, and all four caught by `TestEveryPolicyGrammarCaseIsDecidedTheSameWay`, which reads the vectors. That is the cross-implementation contract failing, not an in-repo assertion.

- **6.5k-iv-a — the served log's three checks, two of which were pinned by nothing.**
  - **D11: every `served-bad` case was refused by the wrong rule.** §6 states two: rebuild the tree from the entry bundles and compare that head with the signed checkpoint, and check the served hash tiles against the same tree. Tamper with an entry and its *leaf hash* changes too, so the tile disagrees and the tile rule fires first — which is why `V154_NoRebuild`, a reader that never compares the rebuilt head with the checkpoint at all, passed all 236 checks. The sentence "that comparison is what binds the entries to the head" was load-bearing for nothing.
  - **`consistent-tampered-log` changes both together.** The level-0 tile is recomputed over the tampered entries, so the served directory agrees with itself and describes a tree the checkpoint is not signed over. The case replaces two paths at once, which `cases.json` now expresses with a `files` array, and the test asserts the property rather than trusting the generator: the replacement tile must be exactly the leaf hashes of the replacement bundle, or the tile rule could still refuse it and nothing would have changed.
  - **The root line needed a negative that a lax reader accepts, not one it refuses.** A root line one byte *short* is caught by the rebuild — the truncated value is not the tree's head — so the length rule would have stayed refused-but-not-pinned. `17-root-wrong-length` is one byte *long*, its first 32 bytes the honest root: a reader that takes 32 and reads on matches the tree and accepts a checkpoint whose root line is not a SHA-256 hash. §6 now also says the encoding is standard base64 with padding.
  - **Two controls, two reds, one vector each.** Deleting the head comparison fails `consistent-tampered-log` and nothing else; accepting a longer root fails `17-root-wrong-length` and nothing else. That is D-044 in both directions.
  - **The third check cannot be vectored and now says so.** §10 takes the served branch's root at `at` "recomputed from the entries, never from a supplied proof". No file supplies one — a `detect/` pair serves both branches with their own tiles — and reaching the clause needs a served log carrying a proof, which `tlog-tiles` does not serve. It joins §12.1's `What is not vectored` list, where a rule that looks covered and is not belongs.

- **6.5k-iv-b — §7's five splits, three vectored and two admitted.**
  - **D13: a restatement that says it is sufficient, and left five readings alive.** Split a signature line at its **last** space; accept **unpadded** base64; accept a payload **longer** than 68 or 76 and verify the prefix; end the text at the **last** blank line; require no signature line at all. Every one passed all 236 checks.
  - **`V206` is the one with teeth.** A payload one byte long still verifies under a prefix-reading verifier, so one checkpoint has *unboundedly many* byte-distinct forms that are all valid. Two nodes gossiping that head then disagree about its bytes, and anything keyed on them — a cache, a de-duplication set, §6's OpenTimestamps digest over the body — is keyed on something an attacker picks. §7 now says **exactly** 68 and **exactly** 76, and says why.
  - **Three negatives, each the honest checkpoint with one signature line rewritten.** `18-signature-name-with-space` (a name with a space in it: split at the first space the payload is not base64, split at the last it is a signature from a name nobody lists, which §10 *ignores* rather than refuses), `19-signature-unpadded-base64` and `20-signature-payload-too-long`. Each test proves the line is well-formed under the lax reading before asserting that this one refuses it — for `20`, by verifying the first 68 bytes against the log key over the note text.
  - **The blind model had to grow with them.** `TestTheSignatureBlindSetIsComputedNotAsserted` computes which cases a reader that never calls Ed25519 accepts, and its model checked only the em dash and the block's newline — so it declared all three blind-accepted. They are not: the base64 and the length need no key either. "Blind" means it does not *verify* a signature, not that it does not *parse* one. That mistake is the same one this test exists to prevent, caught by the test itself.
  - **Two splits no file can separate, now written down.** A blank line inside a note's text is refused by the line count under **both** readings, because every note text in Norn is a three-line checkpoint body or a four-line receipt. A note with no signature lines is refused by §10's "at least one such line MUST be present" before §7's framing is reached. Both join §12.1's `What is not vectored`.
  - **Three controls, three reds, one vector each.**

- **6.5k-iv-c — a cosignature line names a witness by **name and key id**, and the vector is a positive.**
  - **D16: §10 settled this for the log's own key and left the witness half saying only "naming".** §7 makes the key id bind the name, the algorithm and the public key — so a line carrying a listed witness's *name* under somebody else's *key* is either a stranger's line to ignore or that witness's line failing to verify. Run 10's `V236_CosigNoKeyIDMatch` took the second reading and passed all 236 checks, because `served-extra-witness/` uses a stranger with a different **name** and cannot reach the question.
  - **The reading matters because the wrong one is a veto.** Cosign someone's head under a name you do not hold and every node matching on the name alone refuses it — including nodes that have never heard of you, for a witness that never signed anything. One party denies a checkpoint to the whole network with a line nobody asked for.
  - **`served-name-collision/` is the separating file, and it MUST be *accepted*.** The served head plus one more line carrying `norn-witness/pi`'s name under the **same key `served-extra-witness/` uses**. That pairing is §7's point made twice: one key, two names, two key ids, both lines ignored — one because the name is unknown, this one because the id is. The test asserts the shape before the verdict: two lines carry the name, exactly one carries the key id.
  - **One control, one red.** Dropping the key-id half of the match refuses the head, and nothing else in the suite moves.
  - Also fixed: `served-name-collision` had to join the Makefile's `GO_VECTOR_DIRS`, or `make vectors-check` reports the Go-generated directory as a difference from the Python reference — which it did, immediately.

- **6.5k-v-a — three §9.1 MUSTs about the log key, each deletable with the suite still green.**
  - **The cause was the same in all three, and it was the *policy*.** `11-log-key-name-mismatch`, `13-origin-only` and `16-log-key-is-a-cosignature-key` damage `log_key` or `origin` and left the policy carrying the *original* key — so §9.1's correspondence rule ("a `log` line, if present, MUST be `norn.trust.log_key`") refused the file before the rule the case is named after was ever reached. `13`'s own comment claimed "ONLY 'origin derives from net' can reject this", and it was wrong.
  - **`V90`/`V93` is the pair run 10 called the sharpest.** Two separate MUSTs, each with no observable effect, and only deleting *both* lets `04-origin-mismatch` through — because `04` breaks both at once and §12.1 counted it among its 38 refusals without noticing.
  - **Each case now carries its damaged key in its policy too.** Three controls, three reds, **one vector each**: dropping the derivation fails `13` alone, dropping the note-name equality fails `11` alone, dropping the cosignature-key ban fails `16` alone — all three in `TestEveryDamagedAnchorIsRejected`, which reads the manifest.
  - **A guard from the previous checkpoint fired here.** `make vectors` refuses to run with a vector directory in neither `OWNED` nor `FOREIGN`, and `served-name-collision` was in neither — 6.5k-iv-c ran `vectors-check` and `vectors-log` but not `vectors`, so the gap surfaced one checkpoint late. It is registered now, and the lesson is that the four checks are not interchangeable.

- **6.5k-v-b — three rules no artifact can reach, and each one was checked before it was admitted.**
  - **The admission had to be earned, not assumed.** Run 10 said each of these is deletable with the suite still green; I deleted each one and watched. §4.3's per-key de-duplication in signature counting: green — `sigs` is sorted by `kid` with no repeats and `kid` is a function of the key, so it can never fire twice. §5.1's entry-0 order: green with **four** of its steps rearranged, because every one of them aborts with `bad-genesis` and §5.2's "the first matching step decides the reason code" simply does not apply to a step list with one reason code.
  - **The third came back red, and that is the more interesting answer.** Counting an unverified line's timestamp "in the moment before that refusal" is caught — by `TestTheForgedCosignatureIsCountedButNotVerified`, which asserts a refused checkpoint yields *no* cosignatures. But that is a property of one function's **return value**, not of any file: a second implementation returning the timestamp and then honouring the error is indistinguishable on every artifact here. So §12.1 says exactly that, rather than calling it covered or calling it dead.
  - **§5.1 stopped implying a guarantee it cannot give.** "In order:" now carries "every failure here is `bad-genesis`, so no artifact distinguishes one arrangement of these steps from another" — the reader is told which of the two orderings in this document is load-bearing and which is editorial.
